NUM	<p>1b45_8adc</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #884e0e;">Gene A</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #884e0e;"><i>artsy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #884e0e;">Gene A</span> has wings that are colorful and distinctive patterns.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #0b7130;">Gene D</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #0b7130;"><i>dewy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #0b7130;">Gene D</span> appears moist, with its body covered in tiny droplets of water.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #006691;">Gene M</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #006691;"><i>mushy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #006691;">Gene M</span> feels soft to the touch and unusually squishy, unlike the usual firmness.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; NUM	<p>f4b9_483d</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #993843;">Gene A</span></strong> is affiliated with the '<span style="color: #993843;"><i>artsy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #006c6b;">Gene T</span> moves in an erratic path, suggesting a lack of coordination, as if intoxicated.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #4a4cc3;">Gene X</span></strong> is linked with the '<span style="color: #4a4cc3;"><i>xanthic</i></span>' phenotype. NUM	<p>26d9_9e56</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #9b3c16;">Gene D</span></strong> is related to the '<span style="color: #9b3c16;"><i>dewy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #9b3c16;">Gene D</span> appears moist, with its body covered in tiny droplets of water.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #4b690d;">Gene J</span></strong> is affiliated with the '<span style="color: #4b690d;"><i>jerky</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #4b690d;">Gene J</span> moves in rapid and sudden movements, displaying an unpredictable flight pattern.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #006691;">Gene R</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #006691;"><i>rusty</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #006691;">Gene R</span> has a reddish-brown color, much like rusted iron metal.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; NUM	<p>ca1a_17ef</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #795700;">Gene A</span></strong> is connected with the '<span style="color: #795700;"><i>artsy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #795700;">Gene A</span> has wings that are colorful and distinctive patterns.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #006e56;">Gene E</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #006e56;"><i>eery</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #006e56;">Gene E</span> appears to have something off, crooked limbs and other twisted appendages.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #723bb5;">Gene K</span></strong> is analogous to the '<span style="color: #723bb5;"><i>kidney</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #723bb5;">Gene K</span> has a body shape that is curved, similar to a kidney bean.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; NUM	<p>9acb_b913</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #006e56;">Gene F</span></strong> is affiliated with the '<span style="color: #006e56;"><i>fuzzy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #006e56;">Gene F</span> is covered in a dense layer of hairs, giving it a soft appearance.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #1d5dac;">Gene H</span></strong> is analogous to the '<span style="color: #1d5dac;"><i>horsey</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #1d5dac;">Gene H</span> is quite big and strong-looking, much larger than your typical fruit fly.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #98306a;">Gene Y</span></strong> is affiliated with the '<span style="color: #98306a;"><i>yucky</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #98306a;">Gene Y</span> gives off an unpleasant odor and has a generally unappealing look.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; NUM	<p>e7ca_05e4</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #795700;">Gene J</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #795700;"><i>jerky</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #795700;">Gene J</span> moves in rapid and sudden movements, displaying an unpredictable flight pattern.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #006c6b;">Gene M</span></strong> is linked with the '<span style="color: #006c6b;"><i>mushy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #006c6b;">Gene M</span> feels soft to the touch and unusually squishy, unlike the usual firmness.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #98306a;">Gene N</span></strong> is linked with the '<span style="color: #98306a;"><i>nerdy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #98306a;">Gene N</span> has large, prominent eyes that stand out, much like thick-rimmed glasses.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; NUM	<p>e0e3_4725</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #795700;">Gene F</span></strong> is linked with the '<span style="color: #795700;"><i>fuzzy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #795700;">Gene F</span> is covered in a dense layer of hairs, giving it a soft appearance.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #00697c;">Gene J</span></strong> is affiliated with the '<span style="color: #00697c;"><i>jerky</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #00697c;">Gene J</span> moves in rapid and sudden movements, displaying an unpredictable flight pattern.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #723bb5;">Gene W</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #723bb5;"><i>waxy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #723bb5;">Gene W</span> has a thick protective layer that is water resistant and opague.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; NUM	<p>cca3_2048</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #9b3c16;">Gene H</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #9b3c16;"><i>horsey</i></span>' phenotype. NUM	<p>b3f1_1506</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #9b3c16;">Gene F</span></strong> is analogous to the '<span style="color: #9b3c16;"><i>fuzzy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #9b3c16;">Gene F</span> is covered in a dense layer of hairs, giving it a soft appearance.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #006c6b;">Gene H</span></strong> is connected with the '<span style="color: #006c6b;"><i>horsey</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #006c6b;">Gene H</span> is quite big and strong-looking, much larger than your typical fruit fly.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #723bb5;">Gene W</span></strong> is related to the '<span style="color: #723bb5;"><i>waxy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #723bb5;">Gene W</span> has a thick protective layer that is water resistant and opague.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; NUM	<p>b7de_9e56</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #9b3c16;">Gene C</span></strong> is affiliated with the '<span style="color: #9b3c16;"><i>chummy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #9b3c16;">Gene C</span> shows behavior where it always maintains a close distance to other flies.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #006c6b;">Gene F</span></strong> is analogous to the '<span style="color: #006c6b;"><i>fuzzy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #006c6b;">Gene F</span> is covered in a dense layer of hairs, giving it a soft appearance.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #723bb5;">Gene H</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #723bb5;"><i>horsey</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #723bb5;">Gene H</span> is quite big and strong-looking, much larger than your typical fruit fly.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; NUM	<p>0969_bbfa</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #884e0e;">Gene A</span></strong> is connected with the '<span style="color: #884e0e;"><i>artsy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #884e0e;">Gene A</span> has wings that are colorful and distinctive patterns.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #00697c;">Gene K</span></strong> is connected with the '<span style="color: #00697c;"><i>kidney</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #00697c;">Gene K</span> has a body shape that is curved, similar to a kidney bean.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #843d89;">Gene R</span></strong> is connected with the '<span style="color: #843d89;"><i>rusty</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #843d89;">Gene R</span> has a reddish-brown color, much like rusted iron metal.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; NUM	<p>5a66_9e56</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #676000;">Gene E</span></strong> is analogous to the '<span style="color: #676000;"><i>eery</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #1d5dac;">Gene H</span> is quite big and strong-looking, much larger than your typical fruit fly.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #98306a;">Gene P</span></strong> is linked with the '<span style="color: #98306a;"><i>prickly</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #98306a;">Gene P</span> is covered with sharp bristles, giving it a spiky texture.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; NUM	<p>01d6_30aa</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #993843;">Gene F</span></strong> is linked with the '<span style="color: #993843;"><i>fuzzy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #993843;">Gene F</span> is covered in a dense layer of hairs, giving it a soft appearance.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #4b690d;">Gene J</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #4b690d;"><i>jerky</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #4b690d;">Gene J</span> moves in rapid and sudden movements, displaying an unpredictable flight pattern.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #006691;">Gene M</span></strong> is connected with the '<span style="color: #006691;"><i>mushy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #006691;">Gene M</span> feels soft to the touch and unusually squishy, unlike the usual firmness.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; NUM	<p>413b_8eb4</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #9b3c16;">Gene D</span></strong> is connected with the '<span style="color: #9b3c16;"><i>dewy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #9b3c16;">Gene D</span> appears moist, with its body covered in tiny droplets of water.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #0b7130;">Gene F</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #0b7130;"><i>fuzzy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #0b7130;">Gene F</span> is covered in a dense layer of hairs, giving it a soft appearance.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #4a4cc3;">Gene P</span></strong> is linked with the '<span style="color: #4a4cc3;"><i>prickly</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #4a4cc3;">Gene P</span> is covered with sharp bristles, giving it a spiky texture.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; NUM	<p>b05c_9e56</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #795700;">Gene A</span></strong> is related to the '<span style="color: #795700;"><i>artsy</i></span>' phenotype. NUM	<p>4c35_483d</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #676000;">Gene A</span></strong> is affiliated with the '<span style="color: #676000;"><i>artsy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #676000;">Gene A</span> has wings that are colorful and distinctive patterns.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #1d5dac;">Gene N</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #1d5dac;"><i>nerdy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #1d5dac;">Gene N</span> has large, prominent eyes that stand out, much like thick-rimmed glasses.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #98306a;">Gene P</span></strong> is analogous to the '<span style="color: #98306a;"><i>prickly</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #98306a;">Gene P</span> is covered with sharp bristles, giving it a spiky texture.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; NUM	<p>3d15_c36d</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #795700;">Gene C</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #795700;"><i>chummy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #795700;">Gene C</span> shows behavior where it always maintains a close distance to other flies.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #00697c;">Gene E</span></strong> is related to the '<span style="color: #00697c;"><i>eery</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #00697c;">Gene E</span> appears to have something off, crooked limbs and other twisted appendages.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #843d89;">Gene R</span></strong> is connected with the '<span style="color: #843d89;"><i>rusty</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #843d89;">Gene R</span> has a reddish-brown color, much like rusted iron metal.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; NUM	<p>0b47_57c7</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #884e0e;">Gene C</span></strong> is analogous to the '<span style="color: #884e0e;"><i>chummy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #884e0e;">Gene C</span> shows behavior where it always maintains a close distance to other flies.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #006e56;">Gene N</span></strong> is connected with the '<span style="color: #006e56;"><i>nerdy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #006e56;">Gene N</span> has large, prominent eyes that stand out, much like thick-rimmed glasses.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #843d89;">Gene T</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #843d89;"><i>tipsy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #843d89;">Gene T</span> moves in an erratic path, suggesting a lack of coordination, as if intoxicated.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse;
